<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

~~~~
### D33. Approval bound to version and content hash

`content_hash` is the SHA-256 of a canonical encoding: a fixed-order JSON
struct with a format version, the normalized segment rule, the channels in
canonical order, and the three message fields. The brief, counts, version,
and status are not part of it. Only the backend computes it.

Every write goes through `Store.Update(id, fn)`. It takes a row lock
(`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`), runs the domain decision, and writes with `WHERE
version = $locked`. Edits and approval must name the version the client
read (`version_conflict` otherwise). Approval must also name the content
hash the reviewer saw (`content_changed`), and the stored hash is
recomputed to detect tampering. Inside the same transaction approval:

1. re-parses the stored rule through the segment validator;
2. re-runs the content rules for the selected channels;
3. checks every limit with the longest real `first_name` and `city` among
   each selected channel's consented recipients, read in the transaction
   with the consent filter;
4. requires at least one selected channel with a consented recipient;
5. writes status, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, and the audit event.

Re-approving with the same hash is a no-op that returns 200, so retries
and double clicks are safe and record exactly one `approved` event.
Concurrent approvals serialize on the row lock (tested with 8 concurrent
callers against Postgres). Generation and the LLM have no path to approval.

Alternative considered: optimistic locking alone (`UPDATE … WHERE version`
without `FOR UPDATE`). The audience checks must see the campaign that will
be approved, and the lock makes that straightforward. The version
predicate stays as a second guard.

### D34. Edits store invalid content; approval is the gate

PATCH rejects only structurally bad input: unknown channels, invalid UTF-8
or NUL, or text over twice the field's limit. Content that breaks a content
rule (over a limit, unknown variable, HTML, empty) is stored and reported
in `validation`. Rejecting it would discard a marketer's work in progress,
and the preview, which renders stored content, could never show what is
wrong with it. Approval re-validates everything. An edit that changes
nothing does not bump the version, so autosave cannot invalidate a
reviewer's approval for no reason.

### D35. SSE over POST for generation

Draft generation is `POST /campaigns/{id}/drafts` with a
`text/event-stream` response, read with `fetch` and a `ReadableStream`.
`EventSource` only does GET and reconnects automatically. A dropped
connection would then silently start another paid model call.
deployment needs authentication, with the authenticated identity replacing
`approved_by`.

### D38. Frontend structure and new dependency `vue-router`

`vue-router` (5.4.0, the official router) provides the list, new-campaign,
and review pages with real URLs, so reloading a review page shows the
persisted state. All API calls go through `src/api`: a shared client with
stable error codes, runtime guards that reject unexpected response shapes,
and a small fetch-based SSE reader. The backend owns status, validation,
rendering, and approval; the UI shows rune counts of the raw text as hints
and the backend's final counts from the preview. Edits autosave with a
600 ms debounce, one request at a time, followed by a preview refresh.
Approve is enabled only when everything is saved and the preview of the
current version passes. It sends one request even on repeated clicks.
No `v-html` (lint rule); all text renders as text.

The mock LLM paces its chunks (`LLM_MOCK_STREAM_DELAY`, default 40 ms), so
streaming is visible in mock mode. Tests set no delay, or a long one to
prove cancellation.
57:### D7. Kafka: `apache/kafka` in KRaft mode + `twmb/franz-go`
58-
59-KRaft needs no ZooKeeper. franz-go is pure Go (no cgo/librdkafka), supports
60-transactions and consumer groups, and is actively maintained.
61-Auto-topic-creation is disabled: topics will be
… [truncated 545 chars]
~~~~

</details>

**🔧 Bash** — Add Milestone 5 decisions to DECISIONS.md

~~~~
cat >> DECISIONS.md <<'MDEOF'

## Milestone 5 — Queue and reliable delivery

### D39. Deliveries materialized at approval, in the approval transaction

Approval's `Write` carries a `DeliveryPlan` (the segment predicate the
approval checks used, the selected channels, `max_attempts`). The campaign
store applies it in the same transaction as the status change: an
`INSERT … SELECT` of every user matching the predicate with granted consent
for the channel, `ON CONFLICT (campaign_id, user_id, channel) DO NOTHING`,
and, in the same statement, one outbox row per delivery created. Approval,
deliveries, and queue messages therefore commit or roll back together, and
a repeated approval creates nothing (tested with 8 concurrent approvals).

The audience is fixed at approval: a user who grants consent later is not
added. Revocation is still honoured at send time (D42).

Alternative considered: materializing in the worker after approval. It
would need its own idempotency and leave approved campaigns without
deliveries after a crash; the single transaction avoids both.

### D40. Transactional outbox, Kafka as transport only

The API never talks to Kafka. The worker's relay locks unpublished outbox
rows (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so several relays split the work), produces
them with acks from all in-sync replicas (franz-go's idempotent producer),
and marks them published in the same transaction. A crash between produce
and commit republishes them: publishing is at least once.

A queue message carries only `{"delivery_id"}`. All state (status,
attempts, lease, next attempt, error code) lives in `deliveries`, so a
duplicate, stale, or replayed message cannot cause a send by itself.
Topics `deliveries.sms` and `deliveries.email` (6 partitions by default)
are created by the worker at startup with `kadm` (new dependency,
`github.com/twmb/franz-go/pkg/kadm`, the admin package of the Kafka client
already in use). Auto-creation stays disabled. Published outbox rows are
purged after 24 hours.

### D41. Exclusive claim with a lease; attempts as the fencing token

Processing starts with one statement:
`UPDATE deliveries SET status = 'processing', attempts = attempts + 1,
locked_until = clock_timestamp() + lease WHERE id = $1 AND status IN
('queued', 'retrying') AND next_attempt_at <= clock_timestamp() AND attempts
< max_attempts RETURNING …`. Concurrent claimers serialize on the row; one
wins (tested with 20 concurrent claims). No row means a duplicate message:
the consumer acknowledges it without doing anything. The first claim of a
campaign also moves it from `approved` to `sending` with an audit event,
in the same statement.

The outcome is written with `WHERE status = 'processing' AND attempts =
$claimed_attempt`. If the lease expired and another worker claimed the row,
the stale worker's outcome is discarded. The resend is safe because it
reuses the idempotency key (D44).

The lease (60 s) must exceed the rate-limit wait plus the provider timeout
(5 s); configuration requires at least twice the provider timeout. A
maintenance loop moves `processing` rows with an expired lease back to
`retrying` (`lease_expired`, attempt still counted), or to `failed`
(`retries_exhausted`) if that was the last attempt. All times use the
database clock (`clock_timestamp()`), never worker clocks.

When a worker is shut down mid-attempt before the provider answers, it
records `retrying` with the attempt given back (`interrupted`), so
shutdowns do not exhaust attempts. A message already sent then is
protected by the idempotency key.

### D42. Consent and content re-checked right before sending

After the rate-limit token is granted, the worker reads, in one query, the
recipient's current consent for the delivery's channel, the address, the
personalization values, and the campaign's approved content. No consent
means `failed (consent_revoked)` without a provider call. The content is
rendered for the recipient and re-validated by `content.RenderMessage`:
length after per
… [truncated 7972 chars]
~~~~

<details><summary>Tool result</summary>

~~~~
### D32. Campaign state machine in the domain

`internal/campaign` owns the statuses `draft → audience_ready →
drafts_ready → approved` and a transition table (`CanTransition`).
`drafts_ready → drafts_ready` is a regeneration. Content and channel edits
are allowed only in `drafts_ready`. Approved campaigns are immutable;
Milestone 5 adds sending states after `approved`. Illegal actions return
typed errors (`invalid_state`, 409). The database repeats the status list
in a CHECK constraint and requires `approved_by` and `approved_at`
exactly when the status is `approved`, so a half-approved row cannot exist
even through a bug.

Creation runs segment extraction first and inserts the campaign directly
in `audience_ready`, with the `created` and `audience_proposed` events in
the same transaction. A failed or unsupported proposal stores nothing, so
the list never fills with dead campaigns. `draft` exists in the state
machine and the audit trail, not as a resting state.
~~~~

</details>

## 🤖 Claude

Milestone 5 is implemented and verified: approving a campaign now queues one delivery per consented recipient and channel, and three workers send them through Kafka to the mock provider without duplicates and under 100/s per channel. `make test` and `make lint` pass (with no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`), and the live-stack checks below met every acceptance criterion. Nothing is committed.

**Before you commit:** `DECISIONS.md` and `README.md` are listed in `.gitignore` (since before this milestone), so the M5 documentation and decisions I wrote won't reach the repo. The case asks for both files, so it's probably worth un-ignoring them. That's your call; I left `.gitignore` alone.

## What was built
- **Approval:** the same transaction that approves also inserts one delivery per consented recipient and channel, plus an outbox row for each. A unique constraint on campaign, user and channel means approving again creates nothing. Campaign status now continues `approved → sending → completed`.
- **Queue:** the worker publishes outbox rows to Kafka (`deliveries.sms` / `deliveries.email`). The consumer commits offsets only after the delivery row is updated, and gives in-flight work half of `SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT` on shutdown.
- **Sending:** the worker claims the row with a lease, takes a rate-limit token, re-reads consent, renders and re-validates the content, then sends with the delivery id as `Idempotency-Key`. The attempt counter blocks a worker whose lease expired from overwriting a newer result.
- **Retries:** network errors, timeouts, 408, 429 and 5xx are retried; other 4xx fail at once. Backoff is exponential with full jitter (1s, ×2, 60s cap), `Retry-After` is honoured as a minimum, and a delivery fails after 5 attempts. Retries go back through the outbox, the claim and the rate limiter.
- **Rate limit:** one token bucket per channel in Postgres, shared by all workers and timed by the database clock. Defaults are 90/s plus a burst of 5, and startup rejects any setting where the two add up to more than 100.
- **Mock provider:**
  - Requires an idempotency key and only accepts the fake recipient ranges.
  - Injects latency, 429s, 5xx, permanent rejections, lost responses and its own 100/s limit, all reproducible with a fixed seed.
  - Serves `/v1/stats` and `/v1/messages`.
- **Frontend:** shows the `sending` and `completed` statuses, and keeps those campaigns read-only.
- **Docs and config:** `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml` and the CLAUDE.md layout section are updated, along with README and DECISIONS.md (entries D39–D47).
- **New dependency:** `franz-go/pkg/kadm`, the admin package of the Kafka client already in use, for creating topics.

## Tests and checks run
- `make test`: every Go package passed (unit and integration with Postgres and Kafka containers), plus 25/25 frontend tests, in about 61s. `make test-unit` and `make lint` (0 issues) also pass.
- New tests cover:
  - backoff and Retry-After parsing
  - error classification and processor outcomes
  - final rendering with Turkish characters and long names
  - mock provider idempotency, the recipient guard and fault determinism
  - exclusive claims under 20 concurrent claimers, and the sent-then-skipped duplicate
  - lease-expiry recovery
  - the rate limit across 4 instances, checked in every one-second window
  - the relay republishing rows
  - end-to-end retry, permanent failure, lost-response, revoked-consent and Retry-After cases
  - a full Kafka pipeline with three workers that also replays every message
- On the live stack (`docker compose up --build -d --wait --scale worker=3`):

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Example campaign | 507 deliveries created; completed in about 24s; 504 sent (matches the provider's count exactly), 3 failed as permanent rejections; 0 duplicate messages; highest rate 94–95/s per channel |
| Approve again | Returned the campaign unchanged; still 507 deliveries and one approval event |
| Kill a worker mid-run | Killed worker-2 with about 2,000 deliveries queued, then restarted it; campaign completed with 2,275 sent, matching the provider's count, and 0 duplicates; highest rate 95/s |
| Graceful stop | All three workers stopped cleanly in about 1s with no error logs |
| Logs | No emails, phone numbers or message text in api, worker or mock-provider logs |

## Code review
I ran the code-review skill, which checks the diff against the project's standards and against the milestone spec. The spec review found no blocking bugs and every required test covered. I fixed two findings:
- **Wasted retry:** if only the campaign-completion check failed, the worker treated the whole delivery as failed and Kafka redelivered a message with nothing left to do. Now it logs a warning and lets the maintenance sweep finish the job (regression test added).
- **Stale text:** DECISIONS.md D32 still described `approved` as the final status.

I left these open:
- **Frontend duplicates a rule:** it decides which statuses are read-only itself, which CLAUDE.md says the backend should own. The fix is an `editable` flag in the API response. This pattern existed before M5.
- **Small duplications:** two identical sleep helpers, and the outbox insert written twice in SQL.
- **No automated kill-worker test:** I only checked that scenario by hand. The full failure suite is M7 work.
- **No status-transition table test for deliveries:** transitions are covered only through the processor tests.

## Remaining risks
These are also listed in README "Known limitations".
- **Not exactly-once:** "no duplicates" depends on the provider honouring the idempotency key. The mock keeps keys in memory, so restarting it mid-campaign removes that protection.
- **Rate is enforced at token time:** the limit applies when a worker takes a token, not when the provider receives the request; the 90 + 5 defaults leave headroom for that jitter.
- **Long database outage:** a worker keeps retrying its current messages and blocks consumer-group rebalancing until Postgres comes back.
- **Repeated restarts:** attempts interrupted by shutdown are given back, so a delivery can get more than 5 provider calls (always with the same key).
- **Lost last attempt:** if a lease expires on the last attempt, the delivery is marked failed even if the provider accepted it.

Suggested commit message:
```
feat(delivery): queue approved campaigns through Kafka with retries, idempotency, and a shared rate limit

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

// Command mock-provider simulates an SMS/email delivery provider for local
// development and tests. It never contacts a real messaging service; see
// internal/mockprovider for the API, the fake-recipient guard, and the
// injected faults.
//
// Usage:
//
//	mock-provider                    run the server
//	mock-provider healthcheck URL    exit 0 if URL returns 200
package main

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/rand"
	"encoding/binary"
	"fmt"
	"log/slog"
	"os"
	"os/signal"
	"syscall"

	"github.com/oguzyildirim/smart-campaign-composer/internal/mockprovider"
	"github.com/oguzyildirim/smart-campaign-composer/internal/platform/httpserver"
	"github.com/oguzyildirim/smart-campaign-composer/internal/platform/logging"
)

const service = "mock-provider"

func main() {
	if len(os.Args) == 3 && os.Args[1] == "healthcheck" {
		if err := httpserver.Probe(context.Background(), os.Args[2]); err != nil {
			fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err)
			os.Exit(1)
		}
		return
	}

	ctx, stop := signal.NotifyContext(context.Background(), os.Interrupt, syscall.SIGTERM)
	err := run(ctx)
	stop()
	if err != nil {
		logging.New(os.Stderr, service, slog.LevelError).Error("mock provider exited with error", "error", err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
}

	seed := cfg.Seed
	if seed == 0 {

		seed = binary.BigEndian.Uint64(b[:])

		PermanentFailureRate: cfg.PermanentFailureRate,
		LostResponseRate:     cfg.LostResponseRate,
		LostResponseDelay:    cfg.LostResponseDelay,
		MaxPerSecond:         cfg.MaxPerSecond,
	}

## 🤖 Claude

I didn't change anything: `mock-provider/main.go` has no compile errors. With the project's Go 1.27.3 toolchain, `go build ./mock-provider/` and `go vet ./mock-provider/` both finish clean, and so did `make lint` and `make test` earlier.

The errors you're seeing most likely come from the editor using the wrong Go version. Your system Go is 1.21.4, `go.mod` requires 1.26 or newer, and the go command quietly downloads 1.27.3 to build. GoLand doesn't do that: if its GOROOT points at 1.21.4, it will report errors that the real build doesn't have. A 1.27.3 toolchain is already on disk, so to fix it:

1. In GoLand, open **Settings → Go → GOROOT**.
2. Choose `~/go/pkg/mod/golang.org/toolchain@v0.0.1-go1.27.3.linux-amd64`, or click **Add SDK → Download** and pick 1.27.3.
3. If the errors only mention `internal/mockprovider` (a new package from this milestone), run **File → Invalidate Caches → Invalidate and Restart** so GoLand re-indexes it.

If they're still there afterwards, paste the exact error text and the line it's on, and I'll fix the code.
